<!-- page 1 -->
NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, October 13, 2020
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, October 13, 2020, in the Supervisors’
Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, Holbrook, Arizona. The Board may vote to meet in a closed executive
session to discuss certain matters as indicated on the following agenda. The Board may meet in executive session pursuant to
A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3) for legal advice on any item.
We continue to maintain physical distancing and will conduct Board of Supervisor meetings virtually by zoom. Board
Members, staff and members of the public may call and participate in the meeting by following these steps:
To Join Zoom meeting now, click here: https://zoom.us/j/98445916352?pwd=RkI4VFZpK2VvWVNxa3I3UzJKU0E2UT09 
 
Web Conference:
Call In Number: 1-346-248-7799 OR 1-669-900-6833 (you may use either teleconference number)
Meeting ID: 984 4591 6352  
Passcode: 004846
If you would like to speak during Call to the Public please take the following actions: 
Email Melissa Buckley at, melissa.buckley@navajocountyaz.gov 1.
Identify the Board of Supervisors' meeting date, the agenda item # and title.2.
Name & Telephone Number 3.
Comments or questions should be sent by October 12, 2020, by 5:00 pm. for the October 13, 2020, Board meeting.4.
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman's discretion.  A copy of the agenda background material
provided to the Board, with the exception of items to be considered in executive session, is available for public inspection at the
Navajo County website, 
https://www.navajocountyaz.gov/Departments/Board-of-Supervisors/Agendas-Actions-and-Minutes-Full
9:00 a.m. Call To Order:  Invocation and Pledge of Allegiance
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for up to
5 minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond to any
comments but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be
placed on a future agenda.
 
 
2. CONSENT AGENDA: These items are considered to be routine or administrative in nature
and will be approved in a single motion. A Board member may request for any item to be
removed from the consent agenda and considered on the regular agenda:
 
 
a. Minutes: September 22, 2020  
 
b. Warrant and Voucher Report over $1000 September 2020  
 
c. Constable Reports: Snowflake Precinct #3, Show Low Precinct #5 and Pinetop-Lakeside
Precinct #6, September 2020

<!-- page 2 -->
d. Approval of 41 Back Tax Land Parcels sold over the counter in the amount of $17,733.31  
 
e. Contracts signed by County Manager pursuant to Board of Supervisors Resolution: CDBG
Project Extension for Snowflake and the Governor's Office of Highway Safety and
Heber/Overgaard Fire District Highway Safety Contract for Pediatric Restrain System
 
 
f. Arizona Internet Crimes Against Children Task Force Intergovernmental Agreement with
Phoenix Police Department in the amount of $5000
 
 
g. Adopt Resolution Number 33-2020 approving a Special Use Permit Extension to operate an
already existing 198' meteorological tower on APN 111-08-002D, a portion of Township 16
North, Range 18E, Section 20 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian
 
 
h. Adopt Resolution Number 34-2020 approving a Special Use Permit Extension to operate an
already existing 198' meteorological tower on APN 111-04-001C, a portion of Township 15
North, Range 18 East, Section 9 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian
 
 
i. Adopt Resolution Number 35-2020 approving a Special Use Permit Extension to operate an
already existing 198' meteorological tower on APN 111-03-001G, a portion of Township 15
North, Range 17 East, Section 13 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian
 
 
j. Adopt Resolution Number 36-2020 approving a Special Use Permit Extension to operate an
already existing 198' meteorological tower on APN 110-07-001E, a portion of Township 16
North, Range 20 East, Section 31 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian
 
 
k. Appointment of Lyle Trone as an Apache County Business Representative on the Local
Workforce Development Board
 
 
l. Adopt Resolution Number 37-2020 formally accepting School House Lane as a primitive
road
 
 
3. NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors
Session: 
 
 
a. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board wishes
to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
1. Memorandum of Agreement with the Show Low Unified School District to formalize the
partnership with Teen Health

<!-- page 3 -->
b. REGULAR AGENDA:  
 
1. Consideration and approval of the 2020 Homeland Security grants awards 200106-02
Training and Exercise in the amount of $62,572; and 200106-01 Citizen Corp Council in the
amount of $5,249. 
 
Presented By: Catrina Jenkins
 
2. Udpate on COVID-19  
Presented By: Jeff Lee
 
4. BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
 
a. Consideration of a Proclamation declaring our gratitude to Douglas A. Ducey, Governor of
the State of Arizona, for the initiative he took in implementing Indigenous Peoples Day. 
 
Presented By: Supervisor Dawnafe Whitesinger
 
5. PUBLIC WORKS:  
 
a. Consideration and possible approval of license extension agreement with AZTEC Land and
Cattle Co. LLC for the Woodruff Pit. 
 
Presented By: John Osgood
 
b. Consideration of the purchase of two 2020 140-15 AWD Caterpillar Motor Graders with a
trade-in of two Motor Graders from Empire Machinery in the amount of $565,117.64.
 
Presented By: John Osgood
 
c. Consideration of the purchase of one 2019 950 GC Wheel Loader with a trade-in of one 1983
950B Caterpillar Wheel Loader from Empire Machinery in the amount of $221,807.66.
 
Presented By: John Osgood
 
d. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and possible adoption Resolution Number 38-2020
approving/denying a Special Use Permit to operate an already existing 198' meteorological
tower on APN 110-02-004A, a portion of Township 15 North, Range 20 East, Section 14 of
the Gila and Salt River Meridian.
 
Presented By: Cody Cooper
 
e. NAVAJO COUNTY FLOOD CONTROL DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session:  
 
1. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board
wishes to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
a. Accept a perpetual Drainage Easement over and across real property in the Sky Hi Retreat
community to Navajo County for drainage, construction and maintenance purposes

<!-- page 4 -->
2. REGULAR AGENDA:  
 
a. Consideration and approval of Navajo County's Community Rating System Annual
Recertification Letter. 
 
Presented By: John Osgood
 
6. BOARD OF EQUALIZATION:  
 
a. Discussion and action regarding Petition for Review of Real Property Valuation on APN
411-45-355 (Van Camp); Swearing in of Assessor, Appraisers and Petitioners.
 
Presented By: Cammy Darris/Melissa Buckley
 
b. Discussion and action regarding Petition for Review of Real Property Valuation on APN
210-09-028B (Park Pineway, LLC); Swearing in of Assessor, Appraisers and Petitioners.
 
Presented By: Cammy Darris/Melissa Buckley
 
c. Consideration and approval of the stipulations on Petition for Review of Real Property on
APN 411-45-106 (Bailey) and APN 212-30-007K (Pinetop Meadows, LLC); Swearing in of
Assessor, Appraisers and Petitioners
 
Presented By: Cammy Darris/Melissa Buckley
 
7. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from County Manager, County Attorney and
Board members.
 
 
 
8. ADJOURN  
 
Dated: October 9, 2020 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Posted: 8:41 a.m. By: MB                       
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board

they are a third of the way through the construction phase. Construction on the water line has been delayed due to the
unforeseen effects of the COVID-19 pandemic on Navajo County. We ask that the contract be extended from a 13.5
month contract to an 18 month contract putting the contract expiration date on 2/1/2021. This would allow the Town to
complete construction and closeout procedures properly and leaves room for further weather delays or COVID related
incidents to occur in the fall and winter of 2020.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. f.        
Meeting Date: 10/13/2020  
Title: Internet Crimes Against Children Task Force 
Submitted For: David Clouse, Sheriff  Submitted By: Cheryl Powell, Deputy Chief 
Department: Sheriff 
Motion before the Board:
Arizona Internet Crimes Against Children Task Force Intergovernmental Agreement with Phoenix
Police Department in the amount of $5000
Background:
The Phoenix Police Department received a grant from the US Department of Justice, Office of
Juvenile Justice and Delinquency Prevention grant regarding internet Crimes Against Children
(ICAC).  Phoenix PD is the primary grantee and will provide financial assistance to the Navajo
County Sheriff's Office for the detection and investigation of sexual exploitation of minors, sexual
assaults and abuse of children facilitated by technology.  The Sheriff's Office Criminal
Investigations unit will work with a multi-agency task force to respond to the ICAC offenses.
The $5000 awarded may be used for training, investigations, computer forensics and public
awareness in connection with Internet Crimes Against Children (ICAC).  It is a reimbursable
program through Phoenix Police Department.  The IGA becomes effective upon the date of the
last signature and remains in effect for three (3) years.

of the existing meteorological tower.

NAVAJO N  
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC WORKS DEPARTMENT 
PLANNING & ZONING 
 
 
STAFF REPORT  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
Hearing Date: October 13, 2020 
Case No:  SUP 20-003, SUP 20-004, SUP 20-005 
Action:   Special Use Permit (SUP) 
Applicant/Owner: Aztec Land and Cattle Company, Ltd. (SUP 20-003 & SUP 20-005) &  
   Aztec East Jeffers, LLC (SUP 20-004) 
 
Project Name: Aztec Meteorological Towers 1, 2, & 3 West  
PARCEL INFORMATION  
 Address: No Address 
 
 APN:  111-08-002D, 111-04-001C, 111-03-001G 
 
 Legal Desc: 111-08-002D: Township 16 North, Range 18 East, Section 20 of the Gila and
  Salt River Meridian. 
 
   111-04-001C: Township 15 North, Range 18 East, Section 9 of the Gila and 
  Salt River Meridian. 
 
   111-03-001G: Township 15 North, Range 17 East, Section 13 of the Gila and 
  Salt River Meridian. 
 
 District: III 
 
STATED REASON FOR REQUEST:  
 
The applicant has requested Special Use Permit extensions to operate three (3), separate, already 
existing 198’ meteorological towers. 
 
SURROUNDING USES:

<!-- page 478 -->
111-08-002D 
 
Direction Zoning/Use 
North A-General, Vacant 
East A-General, Vacant 
South A-General, Vacant 
West A-General, Vacant 
 
111-04-001C 
 
Direction Zoning/Use 
North A-General, Vacant 
East A-General, Vacant 
South State Land, Vacant 
West A-General, Vacant 
 
111-03-001G 
 
Direction Zoning/Use 
North A-General, Vacant 
East A-General, Vacant 
South USFS Land, Vacant 
West A-General, Vacant 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
These properties were all previously granted Special Use Permits , on July 14, 2015,  to construct and 
operate meteorological towers via Board of Supervisors Resolutions 19 -15, 20-15, and 21-15.  Due to 
splitting of the subject properties following the granting of Special Use Permits, the subject APNs have 
slightly changed, now having suffixes.  Each Special Use Permit was approved based on the same, 
following conditions:  
 
1. This Special Use Permit shall allow for the installation of one (1) meteorological tower, with a 
maximum height of approximately 198 feet (60 meters). 
 
2. The meteorological tower shall be installed only in the general location as indicated on the approved 
site plan, except as may otherwise be approved by the Navajo County Public Works Department. 
 
3. The meteorological tower shall be located so it will not fall onto an adjacent property nor another 
structure. 
 
4. The minimum setback for the meteorological tower, as measured from the property line or any 
public roadway, shall be 1.5 times the total tower height. 
 
5. The meteorological tower shall include the installation of low -intensity, red blinking lighting for 
night-time visibility.  The use of strobes or strobe -type lighting is prohibited.  The meteorological 
tower shall also be painted in alternating orange/red and w hite colors in conformance with FAA 
requirements.

<!-- page 479 -->
6. A Floodplain Use Permit shall be obtained by the developer at the time a Building Permit is secured 
if the tower is found to be located in a Special Flood Hazard Area. 
 
7. Building Permits are required for the meteorological tower and any accessory uses (such as fences, 
etc.), and the issuance of a permit shall be acquired prior to placement of any meteorological tower. 
 
8. Prior to acquiring a Building Permit, and as development progresses, the applicant shall c onsult 
with and obtain written comments from the Arizona Game & Fish Department and the U.S. Fish & 
Wildlife Service (Flagstaff Ecological Services Field Office) to select a meteorological tower site 
that avoids habitat features that congregate wildlife and regarding measures to minimize the impact 
on birds and wildlife of the facilities and operations authorized by this Special Use Permit.  The 
applicant shall give due consideration to the concerns and recommendations of these agencies and 
shall give due consideration to the concerns and recommendations of these agencies and shall use 
all reasonable efforts to satisfy their concerns and to minimize the impact on birds and wildlife, 
including the possible placement of Bird Flight Diverters on the guy wires and acoustic monitoring 
stations on or near the meteorological tower.  The location and construction of facilities shall be 
generally consistent with the Arizona Game & Fish Department’s “Guidelines for Reducing Impacts 
to Wildlife from Wind Energy Developm ent in Arizona” and shall incorporate best available 
technologies and industry practices to minimize the impact on birds and wildlife. 
 
9. Prior to acquiring a Building Permit, the applicant shall provide latitude and longitude coordinates 
(North American Datum of 1983) of the final site of the meteorological tower to the Navajo County 
Public Works Department and to the Arizona Game & Fish Department. 
 
10.  Prior to the issuance of any building permits for the meteorological tower, the developer shall 
submit to the Navajo County Public Works Department information regarding the manufacturer of 
the tower, structural information, information regarding who to contact in the event of an emergency 
or any problems with the tower, and information regarding how data is collected and transmitted. 
 
11. Development and use of the site shall include efforts, such Best Management Practices, to reduce 
& mitigate dust created by this project.  Efforts shall also be made to reduce the removal of 
vegetation during construction and use of the site and any roadways that provide access to the site.  
Upon the removal of the meteorological tower and associated structures and improvements, the site 
and any roadways that provide access to the site shall be restored and re -vegetated to their 
current/native condition. 
 
12. Construction of the meteorological tower shall commence within twelve (12) months following 
approval by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors.  Failure to do so may result in a review by 
the Board of Supervisors for possible revocation of the Special Use Permit. 
 
13. This Special Use Permit and the rights granted herein shall expire sixty (60) months (5 years) from 
the date of Board of Supervisors approval, unless an extension is granted by the Board of 
Supervisors upon a recommendation by the Planning & Zoning Commission.  Application for an 
extension shall be made no later than 90 days before the date of expiration.  If timely application is 
made, the expiration shall be tolled until a final decision by  the Board of Supervisors.  Upon the 
expiration of this Special Use Permit, the Meteorological Tower and associated structures and 
improvements shall be removed, and the site and any roadways that provide access to the site shall 
be restored and re-vegetated to their prior condition, within 60 days.  Failure to accomplish removal 
and restoration within 60 days shall be a nuisance per se and a violation of the Zoning Ordinance, 
subject to enforcement action in accordance with Articles 24 and 27 of said ordinance.

<!-- page 480 -->
At the Planning and Zoning Commission hearing on July 16, 2020, SUP 20-003, 20-004, and 20-005 
were recommended for approval by the Comm ission.  This recomme ndation was subject to the 
conditions outlined i n this report as well as the added condition that Game and Fish b e contacted for 
comments to clarify if they would be in favor or against the proposed SUP s.  Game and Fish was 
contacted and noted that they have no concerns with the proposed SUPs. 
 
PROJECT DESCRIPTION:  
 
The three, 198’ meteorological towers already exist on each of the three properties listed in this report.  
These towers are intended to collect wind speeds, solar intensity, and climate related data.  This data is 
to be used by the owners to determine weather and climate patterns on the properties.  There are not 
currently any development plans for alternate uses of the property other than already existing 
agricultural/grazing uses.  The collection of the data from the towers is to be used in future company 
analysis and land use planning. 
 
ZONING DISTRICTS: 
ARTICLE 3 – (A-GENERAL) – GENERAL ZONING DISTRICT  
Section 301 – Purpose.  
 
The main purpose of this A -General Zone is to provide for all the unincorporated area of the County, 
except land within the boundary of a reservation and outside the authority of Navajo County not 
otherwise designated for some other specific zone, to be included in the “A -General Zone,” by this 
Ordinance.  No subdividing shall be conducted or appr oved in the “A-General Zone,” without prior re-
zoning of the land so intended.  Uses permitted in the “A -General Zone,” include farm and non -farm 
residential uses, farms, recreational, institutional, commercial and industrial uses as specifically listed in 
this Article.  Other uses may be permitted as Special Uses under Article 20. 
 
ZONING ORDINANCE: 
Article 20 – Special Uses and Planned Unit Developments 
 
Section 2001 – Special Uses 
 
14. Experimental and Proving Grounds 
 
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
 
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes 
and Article 29.  
 
This item has been noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes and Article 
29, as required. 
 
2. The proposal is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan. 
 
These proposals comply with all relevant goals and policies of Navajo County’s Comprehensive 
Plan.

<!-- page 481 -->
3. The proposed use is compatible with the permissible uses in the zoning district in which the 
property is located. 
 
The proposed uses should not have incompatibility issues with other permissible uses in the 
zoning district in which the property is located based on its location. 
 
4. The proposed use is compatible with the current and likely future uses of properties in the 
vicinity of the proposed use. 
 
The properties in the vicinity of these proposals appear to be mainly utilized for cattle grazing.  
Given the size of these properties and the relatively small footprint of the meteorological towers, 
there should be no future compatibility issues between uses on these properties. 
 
5. The proposed use will have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general 
welfare of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use. 
 
The proposed use s should have minimal adverse e ffects on the public health, safety, and general 
welfare of properties in its vicinity.  
 
 
PUBLIC WORKS AND OTHER COUNTY DEPARTMENT’S COMMENTS: 
COUNTY ATTORNEY:  
No objection, as long as the applicant complies with all rules and regulations of Navajo County and 
the State of Arizona. 
Initial: B.S.C.  Date:   
      
ENGINEERING AND TRAFFIC: 
Engineering staff has reviewed the proposed Special Use Permit and has no Engineering objections.

Re: Aztec Meteorological Towers 1 -5, Navajo County SUP 20 -003, 20-004, 20-005, 20-006, 
and 20-007  
 
Dear Ms. Nejad, 
 
The Arizona Game and Fish Department (Department) appreciates that Navajo County reached 
out to the Department in regards to the renewal of Aztec Land and Cattle Company Ltd. special 
use permits for five Meteorological Towers (MET) in Nava jo County. The Department 
recognizes wind energy as a viable source of clean and renewable energy and with proper site 
placement and safeguards, the benefits of utilizing wind energy outweigh the potential for 
negative effect to wildlife populations. Simil arly, the Department believes that wind can be a 
viable option for energy, although specific sites may have an increased potential for negative 
impacts to certain breeding, migratory and wintering species. 
 
The Department encourages Aztec Land and Cattle C ompany Ltd, to obtain a copy of the 
Department’s wind development guidelines at: https://s3.amazonaws.com/azgfd-portal-
wordpress/PortalImages/files/wildlife/planningFor/wildlifeFriendlyGuidelines/RevisedAZWind
GuidelinesOctober2012.pdf, which provides guidance on reducing impacts to birds and bats. 
 
Department staff routinely conduct low -level aerial flight’s as part of wildlife population 
management duties, therefore the Department recommends sharing the MET tower locations 
with Department personnel when the towers are constructed and, similarly, when they are 
removed.  Having location da ta for MET tower locations will aid in allowing low -level flying 
aircraft avoid the tower. If a tower has guy wires it is recommended to have a minimum of four 
Aircraft Warning Markers (AWM’s) placed 10 meters below the apex. AWM’s should be 
recognizable f rom a distance of at least 4,000 feet (1219m) in clear air and visible from all 
directions. 
 
The Department appreciates the opportunity to provide comments on Navajo County’s Special 
Use Permitting process. The Department would like to continue to work wit h Aztec Land and 
Cattle Company Ltd and Navajo County on the scope of work moving forward. If these MET 
towers eventually indicate that a site is appropriate for wind energy development, the 
Department and the US Fish and Wildlife Service should be coordin ated  with during the early

<!-- page 484 -->
Aztec Meteorological Towers 1-5, Navajo County SUP 20-003, 20-004, 20-005, 20-006, and 20-007  
August 17, 2020 
Page 2 
planning stages to ensure that any impacts to birds, bats, wildlife migration and populations are 
avoided and/or mitigated for as much as possible. Please contact me with any questions, 
concerns, or requests for further information. Thank you. 
 
Sincerely, 
 
            Evan Lautzenheiser

STAFF REPORT  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
Hearing Date: October 13, 2020 
Case No:  SUP 20-003, SUP 20-004, SUP 20-005 
Action:   Special Use Permit (SUP) 
Applicant/Owner: Aztec Land and Cattle Company, Ltd. (SUP 20-003 & SUP 20-005) &  
   Aztec East Jeffers, LLC (SUP 20-004) 
 
Project Name: Aztec Meteorological Towers 1, 2, & 3 West  
PARCEL INFORMATION  
 Address: No Address 
 
 APN:  111-08-002D, 111-04-001C, 111-03-001G 
 
 Legal Desc: 111-08-002D: Township 16 North, Range 18 East, Section 20 of the Gila and
  Salt River Meridian. 
 
   111-04-001C: Township 15 North, Range 18 East, Section 9 of the Gila and 
  Salt River Meridian. 
 
   111-03-001G: Township 15 North, Range 17 East, Section 13 of the Gila and 
  Salt River Meridian. 
 
 District: III 
 
STATED REASON FOR REQUEST:  
 
The applicant has requested Special Use Permit extensions to operate three (3), separate, already 
existing 198’ meteorological towers. 
 
SURROUNDING USES:

<!-- page 497 -->
111-08-002D 
 
Direction Zoning/Use 
North A-General, Vacant 
East A-General, Vacant 
South A-General, Vacant 
West A-General, Vacant 
 
111-04-001C

North A-General, Vacant 
East A-General, Vacant 
South State Land, Vacant 
West A-General, Vacant 
 
111-03-001G 
 
Direction Zoning/Use 
North A-General, Vacant 
East A-General, Vacant 
South USFS Land, Vacant 
West A-General, Vacant 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
These properties were all previously granted Special Use Permits , on July 14, 2015,  to construct and 
operate meteorological towers via Board of Supervisors Resolutions 19 -15, 20-15, and 21-15.  Due to 
splitting of the subject properties following the granting of Special Use Permits, the subject APNs have 
slightly changed, now having suffixes.  Each Special Use Permit was approved based on the same, 
following conditions:  
 
1. This Special Use Permit shall allow for the installation of one (1) meteorological tower, with a 
maximum height of approximately 198 feet (60 meters). 
 
2. The meteorological tower shall be installed only in the general location as indicated on the approved 
site plan, except as may otherwise be approved by the Navajo County Public Works Department. 
 
3. The meteorological tower shall be located so it will not fall onto an adjacent property nor another 
structure. 
 
4. The minimum setback for the meteorological tower, as measured from the property line or any 
public roadway, shall be 1.5 times the total tower height. 
 
5. The meteorological tower shall include the installation of low -intensity, red blinking lighting for 
night-time visibility.  The use of strobes or strobe -type lighting is prohibited.  The meteorological 
tower shall also be painted in alternating orange/red and w hite colors in conformance with FAA 
requirements.

<!-- page 498 -->
6. A Floodplain Use Permit shall be obtained by the developer at the time a Building Permit is secured 
if the tower is found to be located in a Special Flood Hazard Area. 
 
7. Building Permits are required for the meteorological tower and any accessory uses (such as fences, 
etc.), and the issuance of a permit shall be acquired prior to placement of any meteorological tower. 
 
8. Prior to acquiring a Building Permit, and as development progresses, the applicant shall c onsult 
with and obtain written comments from the Arizona Game & Fish Department and the U.S. Fish & 
Wildlife Service (Flagstaff Ecological Services Field Office) to select a meteorological tower site 
that avoids habitat features that congregate wildlife and regarding measures to minimize the impact 
on birds and wildlife of the facilities and operations authorized by this Special Use Permit.  The 
applicant shall give due consideration to the concerns and recommendations of these agencies and 
shall give due consideration to the concerns and recommendations of these agencies and shall use 
all reasonable efforts to satisfy their concerns and to minimize the impact on birds and wildlife, 
including the possible placement of Bird Flight Diverters on the guy wires and acoustic monitoring 
stations on or near the meteorological tower.  The location and construction of facilities shall be 
generally consistent with the Arizona Game & Fish Department’s “Guidelines for Reducing Impacts 
to Wildlife from Wind Energy Developm ent in Arizona” and shall incorporate best available 
technologies and industry practices to minimize the impact on birds and wildlife. 
 
9. Prior to acquiring a Building Permit, the applicant shall provide latitude and longitude coordinates 
(North American Datum of 1983) of the final site of the meteorological tower to the Navajo County 
Public Works Department and to the Arizona Game & Fish Department. 
 
10.  Prior to the issuance of any building permits for the meteorological tower, the developer shall 
submit to the Navajo County Public Works Department information regarding the manufacturer of 
the tower, structural information, information regarding who to contact in the event of an emergency 
or any problems with the tower, and information regarding how data is collected and transmitted. 
 
11. Development and use of the site shall include efforts, such Best Management Practices, to reduce 
& mitigate dust created by this project.  Efforts shall also be made to reduce the removal of 
vegetation during construction and use of the site and any roadways that provide access to the site.  
Upon the removal of the meteorological tower and associated structures and improvements, the site 
and any roadways that provide access to the site shall be restored and re -vegetated to their 
current/native condition. 
 
12. Construction of the meteorological tower shall commence within twelve (12) months following 
approval by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors.  Failure to do so may result in a review by 
the Board of Supervisors for possible revocation of the Special Use Permit. 
 
13. This Special Use Permit and the rights granted herein shall expire sixty (60) months (5 years) from 
the date of Board of Supervisors approval, unless an extension is granted by the Board of 
Supervisors upon a recommendation by the Planning & Zoning Commission.  Application for an 
extension shall be made no later than 90 days before the date of expiration.  If timely application is 
made, the expiration shall be tolled until a final decision by  the Board of Supervisors.  Upon the 
expiration of this Special Use Permit, the Meteorological Tower and associated structures and 
improvements shall be removed, and the site and any roadways that provide access to the site shall 
be restored and re-vegetated to their prior condition, within 60 days.  Failure to accomplish removal 
and restoration within 60 days shall be a nuisance per se and a violation of the Zoning Ordinance, 
subject to enforcement action in accordance with Articles 24 and 27 of said ordinance.

<!-- page 499 -->
At the Planning and Zoning Commission hearing on July 16, 2020, SUP 20-003, 20-004, and 20-005 
were recommended for approval by the Comm ission.  This recomme ndation was subject to the 
conditions outlined i n this report as well as the added condition that Game and Fish b e contacted for 
comments to clarify if they would be in favor or against the proposed SUP s.  Game and Fish was 
contacted and noted that they have no concerns with the proposed SUPs. 
 
PROJECT DESCRIPTION:  
 
The three, 198’ meteorological towers already exist on each of the three properties listed in this report.  
These towers are intended to collect wind speeds, solar intensity, and climate related data.  This data is 
to be used by the owners to determine weather and climate patterns on the properties.  There are not 
currently any development plans for alternate uses of the property other than already existing 
agricultural/grazing uses.  The collection of the data from the towers is to be used in future company 
analysis and land use planning. 
 
ZONING DISTRICTS: 
ARTICLE 3 – (A-GENERAL) – GENERAL ZONING DISTRICT  
Section 301 – Purpose.  
 
The main purpose of this A -General Zone is to provide for all the unincorporated area of the County, 
except land within the boundary of a reservation and outside the authority of Navajo County not 
otherwise designated for some other specific zone, to be included in the “A -General Zone,” by this 
Ordinance.  No subdividing shall be conducted or appr oved in the “A-General Zone,” without prior re-
zoning of the land so intended.  Uses permitted in the “A -General Zone,” include farm and non -farm 
residential uses, farms, recreational, institutional, commercial and industrial uses as specifically listed in 
this Article.  Other uses may be permitted as Special Uses under Article 20. 
 
ZONING ORDINANCE: 
Article 20 – Special Uses and Planned Unit Developments 
 
Section 2001 – Special Uses 
 
14. Experimental and Proving Grounds 
 
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
 
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes 
and Article 29.  
 
This item has been noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes and Article 
29, as required. 
 
2. The proposal is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan. 
 
These proposals comply with all relevant goals and policies of Navajo County’s Comprehensive 
Plan.

<!-- page 500 -->
3. The proposed use is compatible with the permissible uses in the zoning district in which the 
property is located. 
 
The proposed uses should not have incompatibility issues with other permissible uses in the 
zoning district in which the property is located based on its location. 
 
4. The proposed use is compatible with the current and likely future uses of properties in the 
vicinity of the proposed use. 
 
The properties in the vicinity of these proposals appear to be mainly utilized for cattle grazing.  
Given the size of these properties and the relatively small footprint of the meteorological towers, 
there should be no future compatibility issues between uses on these properties. 
 
5. The proposed use will have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general 
welfare of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use. 
 
The proposed use s should have minimal adverse e ffects on the public health, safety, and general 
welfare of properties in its vicinity.

No objection, as long as the applicant complies with all rules and regulations of Navajo County and

STAFF REPORT  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
Hearing Date: October 13, 2020 
Case No:  SUP 20-003, SUP 20-004, SUP 20-005 
Action:   Special Use Permit (SUP) 
Applicant/Owner: Aztec Land and Cattle Company, Ltd. (SUP 20-003 & SUP 20-005) &  
   Aztec East Jeffers, LLC (SUP 20-004) 
 
Project Name: Aztec Meteorological Towers 1, 2, & 3 West  
PARCEL INFORMATION  
 Address: No Address 
 
 APN:  111-08-002D, 111-04-001C, 111-03-001G 
 
 Legal Desc: 111-08-002D: Township 16 North, Range 18 East, Section 20 of the Gila and
  Salt River Meridian. 
 
   111-04-001C: Township 15 North, Range 18 East, Section 9 of the Gila and 
  Salt River Meridian. 
 
   111-03-001G: Township 15 North, Range 17 East, Section 13 of the Gila and 
  Salt River Meridian. 
 
 District: III 
 
STATED REASON FOR REQUEST:  
 
The applicant has requested Special Use Permit extensions to operate three (3), separate, already 
existing 198’ meteorological towers. 
 
SURROUNDING USES:

<!-- page 516 -->
111-08-002D 
 
Direction Zoning/Use 
North A-General, Vacant 
East A-General, Vacant 
South A-General, Vacant 
West A-General, Vacant 
 
111-04-001C 
 
Direction Zoning/Use 
North A-General, Vacant 
East A-General, Vacant 
South State Land, Vacant 
West A-General, Vacant 
 
111-03-001G 
 
Direction Zoning/Use 
North A-General, Vacant 
East A-General, Vacant 
South USFS Land, Vacant 
West A-General, Vacant 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
These properties were all previously granted Special Use Permits , on July 14, 2015,  to construct and 
operate meteorological towers via Board of Supervisors Resolutions 19 -15, 20-15, and 21-15.  Due to 
splitting of the subject properties following the granting of Special Use Permits, the subject APNs have 
slightly changed, now having suffixes.  Each Special Use Permit was approved based on the same, 
following conditions:  
 
1. This Special Use Permit shall allow for the installation of one (1) meteorological tower, with a 
maximum height of approximately 198 feet (60 meters). 
 
2. The meteorological tower shall be installed only in the general location as indicated on the approved 
site plan, except as may otherwise be approved by the Navajo County Public Works Department. 
 
3. The meteorological tower shall be located so it will not fall onto an adjacent property nor another 
structure. 
 
4. The minimum setback for the meteorological tower, as measured from the property line or any 
public roadway, shall be 1.5 times the total tower height. 
 
5. The meteorological tower shall include the installation of low -intensity, red blinking lighting for 
night-time visibility.  The use of strobes or strobe -type lighting is prohibited.  The meteorological 
tower shall also be painted in alternating orange/red and w hite colors in conformance with FAA 
requirements.

<!-- page 517 -->
6. A Floodplain Use Permit shall be obtained by the developer at the time a Building Permit is secured 
if the tower is found to be located in a Special Flood Hazard Area. 
 
7. Building Permits are required for the meteorological tower and any accessory uses (such as fences, 
etc.), and the issuance of a permit shall be acquired prior to placement of any meteorological tower. 
 
8. Prior to acquiring a Building Permit, and as development progresses, the applicant shall c onsult 
with and obtain written comments from the Arizona Game & Fish Department and the U.S. Fish & 
Wildlife Service (Flagstaff Ecological Services Field Office) to select a meteorological tower site 
that avoids habitat features that congregate wildlife and regarding measures to minimize the impact 
on birds and wildlife of the facilities and operations authorized by this Special Use Permit.  The 
applicant shall give due consideration to the concerns and recommendations of these agencies and 
shall give due consideration to the concerns and recommendations of these agencies and shall use 
all reasonable efforts to satisfy their concerns and to minimize the impact on birds and wildlife, 
including the possible placement of Bird Flight Diverters on the guy wires and acoustic monitoring 
stations on or near the meteorological tower.  The location and construction of facilities shall be 
generally consistent with the Arizona Game & Fish Department’s “Guidelines for Reducing Impacts 
to Wildlife from Wind Energy Developm ent in Arizona” and shall incorporate best available 
technologies and industry practices to minimize the impact on birds and wildlife. 
 
9. Prior to acquiring a Building Permit, the applicant shall provide latitude and longitude coordinates 
(North American Datum of 1983) of the final site of the meteorological tower to the Navajo County 
Public Works Department and to the Arizona Game & Fish Department. 
 
10.  Prior to the issuance of any building permits for the meteorological tower, the developer shall 
submit to the Navajo County Public Works Department information regarding the manufacturer of 
the tower, structural information, information regarding who to contact in the event of an emergency 
or any problems with the tower, and information regarding how data is collected and transmitted. 
 
11. Development and use of the site shall include efforts, such Best Management Practices, to reduce 
& mitigate dust created by this project.  Efforts shall also be made to reduce the removal of 
vegetation during construction and use of the site and any roadways that provide access to the site.  
Upon the removal of the meteorological tower and associated structures and improvements, the site 
and any roadways that provide access to the site shall be restored and re -vegetated to their 
current/native condition. 
 
12. Construction of the meteorological tower shall commence within twelve (12) months following 
approval by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors.  Failure to do so may result in a review by 
the Board of Supervisors for possible revocation of the Special Use Permit. 
 
13. This Special Use Permit and the rights granted herein shall expire sixty (60) months (5 years) from 
the date of Board of Supervisors approval, unless an extension is granted by the Board of 
Supervisors upon a recommendation by the Planning & Zoning Commission.  Application for an 
extension shall be made no later than 90 days before the date of expiration.  If timely application is 
made, the expiration shall be tolled until a final decision by  the Board of Supervisors.  Upon the 
expiration of this Special Use Permit, the Meteorological Tower and associated structures and 
improvements shall be removed, and the site and any roadways that provide access to the site shall 
be restored and re-vegetated to their prior condition, within 60 days.  Failure to accomplish removal 
and restoration within 60 days shall be a nuisance per se and a violation of the Zoning Ordinance, 
subject to enforcement action in accordance with Articles 24 and 27 of said ordinance.

<!-- page 518 -->
At the Planning and Zoning Commission hearing on July 16, 2020, SUP 20-003, 20-004, and 20-005 
were recommended for approval by the Comm ission.  This recomme ndation was subject to the 
conditions outlined i n this report as well as the added condition that Game and Fish b e contacted for 
comments to clarify if they would be in favor or against the proposed SUP s.  Game and Fish was 
contacted and noted that they have no concerns with the proposed SUPs. 
 
PROJECT DESCRIPTION:  
 
The three, 198’ meteorological towers already exist on each of the three properties listed in this report.  
These towers are intended to collect wind speeds, solar intensity, and climate related data.  This data is 
to be used by the owners to determine weather and climate patterns on the properties.  There are not 
currently any development plans for alternate uses of the property other than already existing 
agricultural/grazing uses.  The collection of the data from the towers is to be used in future company 
analysis and land use planning. 
 
ZONING DISTRICTS: 
ARTICLE 3 – (A-GENERAL) – GENERAL ZONING DISTRICT  
Section 301 – Purpose.  
 
The main purpose of this A -General Zone is to provide for all the unincorporated area of the County, 
except land within the boundary of a reservation and outside the authority of Navajo County not 
otherwise designated for some other specific zone, to be included in the “A -General Zone,” by this 
Ordinance.  No subdividing shall be conducted or appr oved in the “A-General Zone,” without prior re-
zoning of the land so intended.  Uses permitted in the “A -General Zone,” include farm and non -farm 
residential uses, farms, recreational, institutional, commercial and industrial uses as specifically listed in 
this Article.  Other uses may be permitted as Special Uses under Article 20. 
 
ZONING ORDINANCE: 
Article 20 – Special Uses and Planned Unit Developments 
 
Section 2001 – Special Uses 
 
14. Experimental and Proving Grounds 
 
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
 
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes 
and Article 29.  
 
This item has been noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes and Article 
29, as required. 
 
2. The proposal is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan. 
 
These proposals comply with all relevant goals and policies of Navajo County’s Comprehensive 
Plan.

<!-- page 519 -->
3. The proposed use is compatible with the permissible uses in the zoning district in which the 
property is located. 
 
The proposed uses should not have incompatibility issues with other permissible uses in the 
zoning district in which the property is located based on its location. 
 
4. The proposed use is compatible with the current and likely future uses of properties in the 
vicinity of the proposed use. 
 
The properties in the vicinity of these proposals appear to be mainly utilized for cattle grazing.  
Given the size of these properties and the relatively small footprint of the meteorological towers, 
there should be no future compatibility issues between uses on these properties. 
 
5. The proposed use will have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general 
welfare of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use. 
 
The proposed use s should have minimal adverse e ffects on the public health, safety, and general 
welfare of properties in its vicinity.

Submitted via email to: 
 
Re: Aztec Meteorological Towers 1 -5, Navajo County SUP 20 -003, 20-004, 20-005, 20-006, 
and 20-007  
 
Dear Ms. Nejad, 
 
The Arizona Game and Fish Department (Department) appreciates that Navajo County reached 
out to the Department in regards to the renewal of Aztec Land and Cattle Company Ltd. special 
use permits for five Meteorological Towers (MET) in Nava jo County. The Department 
recognizes wind energy as a viable source of clean and renewable energy and with proper site 
placement and safeguards, the benefits of utilizing wind energy outweigh the potential for 
negative effect to wildlife populations. Simil arly, the Department believes that wind can be a 
viable option for energy, although specific sites may have an increased potential for negative 
impacts to certain breeding, migratory and wintering species. 
 
The Department encourages Aztec Land and Cattle C ompany Ltd, to obtain a copy of the 
Department’s wind development guidelines at: https://s3.amazonaws.com/azgfd-portal-
wordpress/PortalImages/files/wildlife/planningFor/wildlifeFriendlyGuidelines/RevisedAZWind
GuidelinesOctober2012.pdf, which provides guidance on reducing impacts to birds and bats. 
 
Department staff routinely conduct low -level aerial flight’s as part of wildlife population 
management duties, therefore the Department recommends sharing the MET tower locations 
with Department personnel when the towers are constructed and, similarly, when they are 
removed.  Having location da ta for MET tower locations will aid in allowing low -level flying 
aircraft avoid the tower. If a tower has guy wires it is recommended to have a minimum of four 
Aircraft Warning Markers (AWM’s) placed 10 meters below the apex. AWM’s should be 
recognizable f rom a distance of at least 4,000 feet (1219m) in clear air and visible from all 
directions. 
 
The Department appreciates the opportunity to provide comments on Navajo County’s Special 
Use Permitting process. The Department would like to continue to work wit h Aztec Land and 
Cattle Company Ltd and Navajo County on the scope of work moving forward. If these MET 
towers eventually indicate that a site is appropriate for wind energy development, the 
Department and the US Fish and Wildlife Service should be coordin ated  with during the early

<!-- page 522 -->
Aztec Meteorological Towers 1-5, Navajo County SUP 20-003, 20-004, 20-005, 20-006, and 20-007  
August 17, 2020 
Page 2 
planning stages to ensure that any impacts to birds, bats, wildlife migration and populations are 
avoided and/or mitigated for as much as possible. Please contact me with any questions, 
concerns, or requests for further information. Thank you. 
 
Sincerely, 
 
            Evan Lautzenheiser 
Habitat, Evaluation, and Land Specialist, Pinetop (Region I)  
 
Cc:  Dave Dorum, Habitat, Evaluation, and Lands Program Manager 
       Ginger Ritter, Project Evaluation Program Supervisor

8. Prior to acquiring a Building Permit, and as  development progresses, the applicant shall consult 
with and obtain written comments from the Arizona Game & Fish Department and the U.S. Fish & 
Wildlife Service (Flagstaff Ecological Services Field Office) to select a meteorological tower site 
that avoids habitat features that congregate wildlife and regarding measures to minimize the impact 
on birds and wildlife of the facilities and operations authorized by this Special Use Permit.  The 
applicant shall give due consideration to the concerns and recommen dations of these agencies and 
shall give due consideration to the concerns and recommendations of these agencies and shall use 
all reasonable efforts to satisfy their concerns and to minimize the impact on birds and wildlife, 
including the possible placement of Bird Flight Diverters on the guy wires and acoustic monitoring 
stations on or near the meteorological tower.  The location and construction of facilities shall be 
generally consistent with the Arizona Game & Fish Department’s “Guidelines for Reducing Impacts 
to Wildlife from Wind Energy Development in Arizona” and shall incorporate best available 
technologies and industry practices to minimize the impact on birds and wildlife. 
 
9. Prior to acquiring a Building Permit, the applicant shall provide latitude and longitude coordinates 
(North American Datum of 1983) of the final site of the meteorological tower to the Navajo County 
Public Works Department and to the Arizona Game & Fish Department. 
 
10.  Prior to the issuance of any building permits for the meteorological tower, the developer shall 
submit to the Navajo County Public Works Department information regarding the manufacturer of 
the tower, structural information, information regarding who to contact in the event of an emergency 
or any problems with the tower, and information regarding how data is collected and transmitted. 
 
11. Development and use of the site shall include efforts, such Best Management Practices, to reduce 
& mitigate dust created by this project.  Efforts shall also be made to reduce the removal of

<!-- page 536 -->
vegetation during construction and use of the site and any roadways that provide access to the site.  
Upon the removal of the meteorological tower and associated structures and improvements, the site 
and any roadways that provide access to the site shall be restored and re -vegetated to their 
current/native condition. 
 
12. Construction of the meteorological tower shall commence within twelve (12) months following 
approval by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors.  Failure to do so may result in a review by 
the Board of Supervisors for possible revocation of the Special Use Permit. 
 
13. This Special Use Permit and the rights granted herein shall expire thirty-six (36) months (3 years) 
from the date of Board of Supervisors approval, unless an extension is granted by the Board of 
Supervisors upon a recommendation by the Planning & Z oning Commission.  Application for an 
extension shall be made no later than 90 days before the date of expiration.  If timely application is 
made, the expiration shall be tolled until a final decision by the Board of Supervisors.  Upon the 
expiration of th is Special Use Permit, the Meteorological Tower and associated structures and 
improvements shall be removed, and the site and any roadways that provide access to the site shall 
be restored and re-vegetated to their prior condition, within 60 days.  Failure to accomplish removal 
and restoration within 60 days shall be a nuisance per se and a violation of the Zoning Ordinance, 
subject to enforcement action in accordance with Articles 24 and 27 of said ordinance. 
 
APN 110-07-001E was then granted a Special Use  Permit extension on March 24, 2015 via Board of 
Supervisors Resolution 08-15, subject to the following conditions:  
 
1. The extension of time for this Special Use Permit shall expire 120 months after Board of Supervisors 
approval.  If the owner wishes to ext end the length of this permit, they must submit a request for 
extension to the Board of Supervisors prior to the expiration date. 
 
2. All other conditions of the original Special Use Permit shall carry forward and remain applicable 
with this time extension. 
 
At the Planning and Zoning Commission hearing on July 16, 2020, SUP 20 -003, 20-004, and 20-005 
were recommended for approval by the Commission.  This recommendation was subject to the 
conditions outlined in this report as well as the added condition that Game and Fish be contacted for 
comments to clarify if they would be in favor or against the proposed SUPs.  Game and Fish was 
contacted and noted that they have no concerns with the proposed SUPs. 
 
PROJECT DESCRIPTION:  
 
The 198’ foot meteorological tower already exists on site.  This tower is intended to collect wind speeds, 
solar intensity, and climate related data.  This data is to be used by the owners to determine weather and 
climate patterns on the properties.  There are not currently any development plans for alternate uses of 
the property other than already existing agricultural/grazing uses.  The collection of data from the tower 
is to be used in future company analysis and land use planning. 
 
ZONING DISTRICTS: 
ARTICLE 3 – (A-GENERAL) – GENERAL ZONING DISTRICT  
Section 301 – Purpose.

<!-- page 537 -->
The main purpose of this A -General Zone is to provide for all the unincorporated area of the County, 
except land within the boundary of a reservation and outside the authority of Navajo County not 
otherwise designated for some other specific zone, to be included in the “A -General Zone,” by this 
Ordinance.  No subdividing shall be conducted or approved in the “A -General Zone,” without prior re-
zoning of the land so intended.  Uses permitte d in the “A -General Zone,” include farm and non -farm 
residential uses, farms, recreational, institutional, commercial and industrial uses as specifically listed in 
this Article.  Other uses may be permitted as Special Uses under Article 20. 
 
ZONING ORDINANCE: 
Article 20 – Special Uses and Planned Unit Developments 
 
Section 2001 – Special Uses 
 
14. Experimental and Proving Grounds

property is located.

4. The proposed use is compatible with the current and likely future uses of properties in the

The properties in the vicinity of this proposal are mainly utilized for cattle grazing.  Given the size

meteorological tower, there should be no future compatibility issues between uses on these 
properties. 
 
5. The proposed use will have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general

The proposed use should have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general

Submitted via email to: 
 
Re: Aztec Meteorological Towers 1 -5, Navajo County SUP 20 -003, 20-004, 20-005, 20-006, 
and 20-007  
 
Dear Ms. Nejad, 
 
The Arizona Game and Fish Department (Department) appreciates that Navajo County reached 
out to the Department in regards to the renewal of Aztec Land and Cattle Company Ltd. special 
use permits for five Meteorological Towers (MET) in Nava jo County. The Department 
recognizes wind energy as a viable source of clean and renewable energy and with proper site 
placement and safeguards, the benefits of utilizing wind energy outweigh the potential for 
negative effect to wildlife populations. Simil arly, the Department believes that wind can be a 
viable option for energy, although specific sites may have an increased potential for negative 
impacts to certain breeding, migratory and wintering species. 
 
The Department encourages Aztec Land and Cattle C ompany Ltd, to obtain a copy of the 
Department’s wind development guidelines at: https://s3.amazonaws.com/azgfd-portal-
wordpress/PortalImages/files/wildlife/planningFor/wildlifeFriendlyGuidelines/RevisedAZWind
GuidelinesOctober2012.pdf, which provides guidance on reducing impacts to birds and bats. 
 
Department staff routinely conduct low -level aerial flight’s as part of wildlife population 
management duties, therefore the Department recommends sharing the MET tower locations 
with Department personnel when the towers are constructed and, similarly, when they are 
removed.  Having location da ta for MET tower locations will aid in allowing low -level flying 
aircraft avoid the tower. If a tower has guy wires it is recommended to have a minimum of four 
Aircraft Warning Markers (AWM’s) placed 10 meters below the apex. AWM’s should be 
recognizable f rom a distance of at least 4,000 feet (1219m) in clear air and visible from all 
directions. 
 
The Department appreciates the opportunity to provide comments on Navajo County’s Special 
Use Permitting process. The Department would like to continue to work wit h Aztec Land and 
Cattle Company Ltd and Navajo County on the scope of work moving forward. If these MET 
towers eventually indicate that a site is appropriate for wind energy development, the 
Department and the US Fish and Wildlife Service should be coordin ated  with during the early

<!-- page 540 -->
Aztec Meteorological Towers 1-5, Navajo County SUP 20-003, 20-004, 20-005, 20-006, and 20-007  
August 17, 2020 
Page 2 
planning stages to ensure that any impacts to birds, bats, wildlife migration and populations are 
avoided and/or mitigated for as much as possible. Please contact me with any questions, 
concerns, or requests for further information. Thank you. 
 
Sincerely, 
 
            Evan Lautzenheiser

Board of Supervisors Regular 3. b. 1.        
Meeting Date: 10/13/2020  
Title: 2020 SHSGP Awards
Submitted For: Catrina Jenkins, EMP Director 
Submitted By: Kellie Payton, Emergency Management Admin Asst
Department: NC Public Health Services District Division: Emergency Management
Presented By: Catrina Jenkins
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and approval of the 2020 Homeland Security grants awards 200106-02 Training
and Exercise in the amount of $62,572; and 200106-01 Citizen Corp Council in the amount of
$5,249. 
Background:
Navajo County Emergency Management has been awarded two grants this year through the State
Homeland Security Grant Program. The first is the Citizen Corp Council equipment grant for
volunteers, in the amount of $5,249.00. These funds will be allocated to our volunteer teams
(including CERT, VIP, and Medical Reserve Corps) throughout Navajo County that assist our
first response personnel and our communities in times of disaster. This project will support
existing teams with ongoing training and equipment upgrades. Ongoing recruitment, training, and
exercises will provide additional coverage throughout the county. Our goal is to strengthen the
capabilities and skills needed in the event of an emergency.  We have been approved to purchase
the following equipment items: fire extinguishers, tourniquets, gauze, traffic cones, first aid kits,
CPR masks, medical reserve corps response kits, and more.  
The second grant is for Training and Exercise funds, in the amount of $62,572.00. These funds
will be used to train first responders, emergency management, volunteers and partnering agencies
utilizing the approved FEMA classes such as Intermediate and Advanced Incident Command
System, Public Information Officer Training, and Principals of Emergency Management. In
addition, funds will be used to pay for costs associated with exercises used to put into practice
what is learned in the training. Exercises will include utilization of a "whole
community" approach in order to ensure personnel are familiar with neighboring agencies
capabilities. These funds are shared between Navajo, Apache and Coconino Counties and the
partner agencies they support such as fire departments, law enforcement, emergency medical
services, schools, public works, volunteers, healthcare and more. These funds are an integral part
of preparing for, mitigating against, and responding to any emergency events such as natural
disasters or human caused emergency events.
The combined grand total amount awarded is $67,821.00.
Recommendation:

Staff recommends approval.

SUBRECIPIENT AGREEMENT
20-AZDOHS-HSGP-200106-01
Between
The Arizona Department of Homeland Security
And
Navajo County Emergency Management (DUNS# 604353250)
WHEREAS, A.R.S. § 41-4254 charges the Arizona Department of Homeland Security (AZDOHS) with the
responsibility of administering funds. 
THEREFORE, it is agreed that the AZDOHS shall provide funding to the Navajo County Emergency
Management (Subrecipient) for services under the terms of this Agreement (the "Agreement").
I. PURPOSE OF AGREEMENT
The purpose of this Agreement is to specify the rights and responsibilities of AZDOHS in administering
the distribution of homeland security grant funds to the Subrecipient, and to specify the rights and
responsibilities of the Subrecipient as the recipient of these funds.
II. PERIOD OF PERFORMANCE, TERMINATION AND AMENDMENTS
This Agreement shall become effective on October 1, 2020 and shall terminate on September 30,
2021. The obligations of the Subrecipient as described herein will survive termination of this agreement.
III. DESCRIPTION OF SERVICES
The Subrecipient shall provide the services for AZDOHS as set forth in writing in Subrecipient’s grant
application titled: "2020 Navajo County Citizen Corps Program" and funded at $5,249 (as may have
been modified by the award letter).
IV. MANNER OF FINANCING
The AZDOHS shall under the U.S. Department of Homeland Security grant # EMW-2020-SS-00017-S01
and CFDA #97.067:
a. Provide up to $5,249 to the Subrecipient for services provided under Paragraph III.
b. Payment made by the AZDOHS to the Subrecipient shall be on a reimbursement basis only and
is conditioned upon receipt of proof of payment and applicable, accurate and complete
reimbursement documents, as deemed necessary by the AZDOHS, to be submitted by the
Subrecipient. A listing of acceptable documentation can be found at www.azdohs.gov. Payments
will be contingent upon receipt of all reporting requirements of the Subrecipient under this
Agreement.
20-AZDOHS-HSGP-200106-01

    V. FISCAL RESPONSIBILITY It is understood and agreed that the total amount of the funds used under this Agreement shall    be used only for the project as described in the application and award documentation.  Therefore, should the project not be completed, the Subrecipient shall reimburse said funds directly to the AZDOHS immediately.  If the project is completed at a lower cost than the original budget called for, the amount reimbursed to the Subrecipient shall be for only the amount of dollars actually spent by the Subrecipient in accordance with the approved application.  For any funds received under this Agreement for which expenditure is disallowed by an audit exemption or otherwise by the AZDOHS, the State, or Federal government, the Subrecipient shall reimburse said funds directly to the AZDOHS immediately.  VI. FINANCIAL AUDIT/PROGRAMMATIC MONITORING The Subrecipient agrees to comply with the record-keeping requirements and other requirements of A.R.S. 35-214 and 35-215.  a) In addition, in compliance with the Federal Single Audit Act (31 USC 7501-7507) as amended by the Single Audit Act Amendments of 1996 (P.L. 104-156) and 2 C.F.R.  200.501, the Subrecipient must have a Single Audit or program specific audit conducted in accordance with 2 C.F.R. 200 (Uniform Administrative Requirements, Cost Principles, and Audit Requirements for Federal Awards) if the Subrecipient expends more than $750,000 from Federal awards in its previous fiscal year.  If the Subrecipient has expended more than $750,000 in Federal dollars, a copy of the Subrecipient’s single audit or program specific audit report for the previous fiscal year and subsequent fiscal years that fall within the period of performance is due annually to AZDOHS within nine (9) months of the Subrecipient’s fiscal year end.   b) Failure to comply with any requirements imposed as a result of an audit will suspend reimbursement by AZDOHS to the Subrecipient until the Subrecipient is in compliance with all such requirements.  Additionally, the Subrecipient will not be eligible for any new awards until the Subrecipient is in compliance with all such requirements.                                                                                                                                                          c) Subrecipients who do not expend more than $750,000 in Federal dollars in the previous fiscal year and subsequent fiscal years that fall within the period of performance must submit to AZDOHS via audits@azdohs.gov, a statement stating they do not meet the threshold and therefore do not have to complete a single audit or program specific audit.   d) Subrecipient will be monitored periodically by AZDOHS, both programmatically and financially, to ensure that the project goals, objectives, performance requirements, timelines, milestone completion, budgets, and other related program criteria are being met. Monitoring will be accomplished through a combination of office-based reviews and on-site monitoring visits. Monitoring can involve aspects of the work involved under this Agreement including but not limited to the review and analysis of financial, programmatic, equipment, performance, and administrative issues relative to each program and will identify areas where technical assistance and other support may be needed.  Subrecipient shall participate in and cooperate with all such monitoring by AZDOHS, and shall provide access to all personnel, documents, and other records as may be requested from time to time by AZDOHS.  Subrecipient also shall comply with all requests of AZDOHS that AZDOHS deems necessary to assure the parties’ compliance with their obligations under this Agreement.   e) The Subrecipient agrees to cooperate with any assessments, state/national evaluation efforts, or information or data collection requests, including, but not limited to, the provision of any information required for the assessment or evaluation of any activities within this Agreement.

 VII. APPLICABLE STANDARDS AND REGULATIONS The Subrecipient must comply with the applicable Notice of Funding Opportunity (NOFO) and Code of Federal Regulations (C.F.R.) 2 C.F.R. 200.  The NOFO for this program is hereby incorporated into this Agreement by reference.  By accepting this award, the Subrecipient agrees that all allocation and use of funds under this grant will be in accordance with the requirements contained in the NOFO and all other applicable law.     Davis Bacon Act HSGP Program subrecipients using funds for construction projects must comply with the Davis-Bacon Act (40 U.S.C. 3141 et seq.). Subrecipients must obtain written approval from AZDOHS prior to use of any HSGP funds for construction or renovation.  Subrecipients must ensure that their contractors or subcontractors for construction projects pay workers no less than the prevailing wages for laborers and mechanics employed on projects of a character similar to the contract work in the civil subdivision of the state in which the work is to be performed. Additional information regarding compliance with the Davis-Bacon Act, including Department of Labor (DOL) wage determinations, is available from the following website https://www.dol.gov/whd/govcontracts/dbra.htm.   Insurance Coverage The Subrecipient affirms the organization maintain insurance coverage as described in 2 C.F.R. 200.310. The non-Federal (Subrecipient) entity must, at a minimum, provide the equivalent insurance coverage for real property and equipment acquired or improved with Federal funds as provided to property owned by the non-Federal (Subrecipient) entity. Federally-owned property need not be insured unless required by the terms and conditions of the Federal award.  National Incident Management System (NIMS) The Subrecipient agrees to remain in compliance with National Incident Management System (NIMS) implementation initiatives as outlined in the applicable NOFO.  Environmental Planning and Historic Preservation  The Subrecipient shall comply with Federal, State and Local environmental and historical preservation (EHP) regulations, laws and Executive Orders as applicable. See FEMA publication FP 108-023-1, titled “Environmental Planning and Historic Preservation (EHP) Policy Guidance,” available at https://www.fema.gov/media-library-data/1533321728657-592e122ade85743d1760fd4747241776/GPD_EHP_Policy_Final_Amendment_GPD_final_508.pdf.  Subrecipients proposing projects that have the potential to impact the environment, including but not limited to construction of communication towers, modification or renovation of existing buildings, structures and facilities, or new construction including replacement of facilities, must participate in the Federal Department of Homeland Security (DHS)/Federal Emergency Management Agency (FEMA) EHP review process. The EHP review process involves the submission of a detailed project description that explains the goals and objectives of the proposed project along with supporting documentation so that DHS/FEMA may determine whether the proposed project has the potential to impact environmental resources and/or historic properties. In some cases, DHS/FEMA is also required to consult with other regulatory agencies and the public in order to complete the review process. The EHP review process must be completed before funds are released to carry out the proposed project. If ground disturbing activities occur during project implementation, the Subrecipient must ensure monitoring of ground disturbance and if any archeological resources are discovered, the Subrecipient shall immediately cease construction in that area and notify FEMA, AZDOHS and the appropriate State Historic Preservation Office.  DHS/FEMA will not fund projects that are initiated without the required EHP review. In addition, the following provisions must be adhered to:  Consultants/Trainers/Training Providers

Article AB - Limited English Proficiency (Civil Rights Act of 1964, Title VI) The Subrecipient hereby acknowledges and agrees that it must comply with the Title VI of the Civil Rights Act of 1964 (42 U.S.C. 2000d et seq.) prohibition against discrimination on the basis of national origin, which requires that recipients of federal financial assistance take reasonable steps to provide meaningful access to persons with Limited English Proficiency (LEP) to their programs and services. OFFSHORE PERFORMANCE OF WORK PROHIBITED Due to security and identity protection concerns, all services under this Agreement shall be performed within the borders of the United States. AGREEMENT RENEWAL This Agreement shall not bind nor purport to bind the AZDOHS for any contractual commitment in excess of the original Agreement period, which may not be changed except by a writing signed by all parties hereto in conformity with Paragraph XII, AMENDMENTS. RIGHT TO ASSURANCE If the AZDOHS in good faith has reason to believe that the Subrecipient does not intend to, or is unable to perform or continue performing under this Agreement, the AZDOHS may demand in writing that the Subrecipient give a written assurance of intent and ability to perform. FORCE MAJEURE If either party hereto is delayed or prevented from the performance of any act required in this Agreement by reason of acts of God, strikes, lockouts, labor disputes, civil disorder, or other causes without fault and beyond the control of the party obligated, performance of such act will be excused for the period of the delay. PARTIAL INVALIDITY  Any term or provision of this Agreement that is hereafter declared contrary to any current  or future law, order, regulation, or rule, or which is otherwise invalid, shall be deemed stricken  from this Agreement without impairing the validity of the remainder of this Agreement.   XXII. ARBITRATION In the event of any dispute arising under this Agreement, written notice of the dispute must be provided to the other party within thirty (30) calendar days of the events giving the rise to the dispute. Any claim made by or against the State or any of its political subdivisions (including but not limited to AZDOHS) relating to this Agreement shall be resolved through the administrative claims process.  In the event that the parties would otherwise be in court and/or if A.R.S. 12-1518 applies, the parties shall proceed in arbitration through the American Arbitration Association (“AAA”), with the arbitrator to be selected pursuant to AAA rules and the arbitration to be conducted according to the applicable AAA rules, and with the costs of arbitration (including but not limited to the arbitrator’s fees and costs) to be divided 50/50 between the parties, subject to reallocation between the parties by the arbitrator.  In the event that the parties become involved in litigation with each other relating to this Agreement for any reason in any other forum, both parties agree to have any claim(s) resolved in arbitration on the terms set forth in this part XXII.  Any arbitration award may be enforced through the Maricopa County Superior Court or the U.S. District Court located in Phoenix, Arizona.

Dear Chairman Jason Whiting,
The application that your agency submitted to the Arizona Department of Homeland Security (AZDOHS) for
consideration under the Homeland Security Grant Program has been awarded. The project titled "2020
Navajo County Citizen Corps Program" has been partially funded under the 2020 State Homeland
Security Grant Program for $5,249. The grant performance period is October 1, 2020 to September 30,
2021. Your application will be kept on file and may be considered for additional funding if reallocation funding
becomes available. This grant program is part of the U.S. Department of Homeland Security Grant Program
and specifically is awarded under CFDA #97.067 (Catalog of Federal Domestic Assistance). The FFY 2020
federal award date as indicated in the U.S. DHS award package is 9/01/2020 with a total amount of funding of
$25,332,352.00. The Federal Award Identification Number is EMW-2020-SS-00017-S01.
To access your award documentation:
Log-in to https://azdohs.gov/user. A username and password was provided to you/your staff during the
application phase. If you no longer have your username/password, please contact your Strategic Planner for
assistance. Be advised all applications submitted on behalf of your organization, as well as associated award
information, will be viewable and accessible by all authorized users associated with your organization.
To establish acceptance of the award, please follow these instructions:
The following action items must be downloaded, completed, signed and returned to AZDOHS:
1. Project Administration Page - Print and sign one original Project Administration Page.
2. Two Subrecipient Agreements - Print and sign two original Subrecipient Agreements, completing
sections XXXVII and XXXVIII.
3. Environmental and Historic Preservation (EHP) required documentation, if applicable.
These items must be completed and on file at AZDOHS in order for your agency to be eligible for
reimbursement. If all documentation listed in numbers 1, 2, and 3 (if applicable), above is not signed
and received by AZDOHS on or before January 31, 2021, this award is rescinded and the funds will be
reallocated.

Board of Supervisors Regular 4. a.        
Meeting Date: 10/13/2020  
Title: Indigenous Peoples' Day Proclamation
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Board of Supervisors
Presented By: Supervisor Dawnafe Whitesinger
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of a Proclamation declaring our gratitude to Douglas A. Ducey, Governor of the
State of Arizona, for the initiative he took in implementing Indigenous Peoples Day. 
Background:
On September 4, 2020 the Governor of the State of Arizona, Douglas A Ducey declared October
12, 2020 as "Indigenous People's Day" to be served simultaneously with Columbus Day as an
opportunity to celebrate Indigenous heritage and resilience. We are grateful for the initiative that
he took in implementing this day. 
Recommendation: 
Recommend approval. 
Attachments
Indigenous Peoples' Day 
Form Review
Form Started By: Melissa Buckley Started On: 10/07/2020 12:07 PM
Final Approval Date: 10/07/2020

<!-- page 604 -->
Navajo County               
Proclamation 
  
Whereas, the State of Arizona has declared October 12, 2020 as “Indigenous Peoples’ Day”, to be served 
simultaneously with Columbus Day as an opportunity to celebrate Indigenous heritage and resilience; and 
 
Whereas, Indigenous peoples who have inhabited this continent since time immemorial, shared their 
knowledge of the land and its resources, and have continued to play a consequential role in the development 
of the State of Arizona, and the United States; and 
 
Whereas, Navajo County recognizes the historic, cultural contributions and contemporary significance of 
Indigenous people; and 
 
Whereas, Navajo County celebrates a positive relationship with the Indigenous populations within Navajo 
County, and continue to grow in shared progress; and honor and respect the heritage, cultural and economic 
contributions of Indigenous people; and 
 
Whereas, Navajo County rejects oppression against underrepresented groups that perpetuate socioeconomic 
disparities; and 
 
Whereas, Navajo County recognizes that Indigenous people are the first inhabitants of the Americas, including 
lands that later became the United States of America; and 
 
Whereas, we acknowledge historic injustices suffered by Indigenous people.   
 
Now therefore, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors hereby proclaim our gratitude to Douglas A. 
Ducey, Governor of the State of Arizona, for the initiative he took in implementing Indigenous Peoples 
Day.  
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Board of Supervisors on _______, 20__. 
         ____________________________ 
Jason E. Whiting, Chairman 
 
ATTEST: 
_________________________________ 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk of the Board

Board of Supervisors Regular 5. a.        
Meeting Date: 10/13/2020  
Title: Woodruff Pit Aztec Land and Cattle Co License Extension Agreement - District III 
Submitted For: Bill Bess, County Engineer/Assistant Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Bryan Cook, Sr. Engineering Project Manager II
Department: Public Works
Presented By: John Osgood
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and possible approval of license extension agreement with AZTEC Land and Cattle
Co. LLC for the Woodruff Pit. 
Background:
Navajo County and AZTEC Land and Cattle Co, LLC entered into a license
agreement in 2016 which allows the County to remove material from two ridge
lines located within AZTEC Land and Cattle Co's property commonly known as
the Woodruff Pit.  The license expired and both parties have agreed to an
extension through April, 2024.  The material screened and removed from the pit is used by
Public Works road maintenance crews on County maintained gravel roads throughout the region.
As part of this agreement Navajo County has conducted an archeological survey which will be
provided to the landowner. The survey did not find anything of cultural value or of concern to our
continued gravel operations.
Per the agreement, Navajo County will track material removed from the Woodruff pit and pay
$1/cy for the material.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends the approval of the license extension and signing of the agreement by the
Chairman. 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2021
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: NA
Fiscal Impact:
Navajo County pays $1/cy of material removed from the pit; adequate funds are available in the
Public Works HURF road maintenance budget.
