Board of Supervisors Regular 5. b.        
Meeting Date: 10/13/2020  
Title: Purchase of two 2020 140-15 AWD Caterpillar Motor Graders
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Jeanine Carruthers, Deputy Director Administration
Department: Public Works
Presented By: John Osgood
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of the purchase of two 2020 140-15 AWD Caterpillar Motor Graders with a
trade-in of two Motor Graders from Empire Machinery in the amount of $565,117.64.
Background:
The City of Tucson has a contract with Caterpillar, Inc. for the purchase and/or lease of heavy
equipment.  Empire Machinery is a dealer for Caterpillar, Inc. and this contract is available for the
use of public and nonprofit agencies that have entered into a Cooperative Purchasing Agreement
with the City of Tucson's Department of Procurement or registered with the National IPA. 
According to the City of Tucson's website, Navajo County is a Cooperative Partner, and we are
also registered with the National IPA.
Staff feels that the price under this contract is fair and being that Empire is a local dealer in Show
Low we have easy access for any warranty repairs and/or needed parts. If approved, the following
equipment will be traded in: 
1984 140G Caterpillar Motor Grader, S/N 72V08109 (18,000 hours) for $29,400
1992 140G Caterpillar Motor Grader, S/N 72V15385 (16,700 hours=approximately
700,000 miles) for $29,400
The 1984 140G Caterpillar Motor Grader needs aproximately $6,500 worth of work (hydraulic
system and parts are no longer available).  A similar blade in good working condition is selling for
aproximately $30,000.  The 1992 140G Caterpillar Motor Grader needs aproximately $6,500
worth of work (hydraulic system is worn  out and parts are no longer available).  A similar blade
in good working condition is selling for approximately $35,000.
Recommendation: 
These blades are 28-37 years old and not only is it difficult to find repair parts but they utilize
approximately 5 gallons of fuel per hour and the new blades only utilize approximately 2.9
gallons of fuel per hour. Valuable heavy equipment fleet maintenance time and resources are
expended trying to keep the equipment operational and considerable downtime of the equipment
results in reduced productivity.  Therefore, staff is recommending the purchase of the two blades
with a trade-in of two blades as presented.

950B Caterpillar Wheel Loader from Empire Machinery in the amount of $221,807.66.

The City of Tucson has a contract with Caterpillar, Inc. for the purchase and/or lease of heavy
equipment.  Empire Machinery is a dealer for Caterpillar, Inc. and this contract is available for the

Board of Supervisors Regular 5. d.        
Meeting Date: 10/13/2020  
Title: Aztec Met Tower 5 East - District III - Request Approval of Special Use Permit
Submitted For: Berrin Nejad, Community Development Director 
Submitted By: Cody Cooper, Planner I
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Zoning
Presented By: Cody Cooper
Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and possible adoption Resolution Number 38-2020
approving/denying a Special Use Permit to operate an already existing 198' meteorological tower
on APN 110-02-004A, a portion of Township 15 North, Range 20 East, Section 14 of the Gila
and Salt River Meridian.
Background:
This property was previously granted a Special Use Permit on April 23, 2013, subject to
conditions.  One of these conditions included a date upon which the permit would expire or
require an extension.  The applicant did not apply for extension of the use of this meteorological
tower by the set date in the original Special Use Permit.  Because of this, this item must be heard
as a new Special Use Permit application.  The applicant is requesting a new Special Use Permit
that would allow for the use of the existing meteorological tower and would take away any further
expiry dates in the future.  This item was unanimously recommended for approval by the Navajo
County Planning and Zoning Commission subject to conditions outlined in the Staff Report and
Resolution for Approval of the requested Special Use Permit. 
Recommendation: 
Staff and the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission recommend approval of
the Special Use Permit, subject to the conditions stated within the Staff Report.

STAFF REPORT  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
Case No:  SUP 20-007 
Action:   Special Use Permit 
Applicant/Owner: Aztec Land and Cattle Company, Ltd.  
 
Project Name: Aztec Meteorological Tower 5 East  
PARCEL INFORMATION  
 Address: No Address 
 
 APN:  110-02-004A 
 
 Legal Desc: Township 15 North, Range 20 East, Section 14 of the Gila and Salt River  
  Meridian. 
 
 District: III 
 
STATED REASON FOR REQUEST:  
 
The applicant has requested a Special Use Permit to operate an already existing 198’ meteorological 
tower. 
 
SURROUNDING USES:  
 
Direction Zoning/Use 
North RU-20, Vacant 
East RU-20, Vacant 
South RU-20, Vacant 
West RU-20, Vacant 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
APN 110-02-004A was previously granted a Special Use Permit, on April 23, 2013, to construct and 
operate a meteorological tower via Board of Supervisors Resolution 12 -13, subject to the following 
conditions:

<!-- page 631 -->
1. This Special Use Permit shall allow for the installation of one (1) meteorological tower, with a 
maximum height of approximately 198 feet (60 meters). 
 
2. The meteorological tower shall be allowed to occur only in the general location as indicated on the 
approved site plan, except as may otherwise be approved by the Navajo County Public Works 
Department. 
 
3. The meteorological tower shall be located s o it will not fall onto an adjacent property nor another 
structure. 
 
4. The minimum setback for the meteorological tower shall be 1,000’ from the project boundary, and 
750’ from any public roadway. 
 
5. The meteorological tower shall include the installation of l ow-intensity, red blinking lighting for 
night-time visibility.  The use of strobes or strobe -type lighting is prohibited.  The meteorological 
tower shall also be painted in alternating orange/red and white colors in conformance with FAA 
requirements. 
 
6. The meteorological tower and all guy -wire attachments/footings shall be enclosed by a security 
fence with a minimum height of 6 -feet and a maximum height of 8-feet (excluding barbed wire or 
cyclone wire).  Information regarding who to contact in the event of an emergency or any problems 
with the tower or its associated uses & structures shall be placed upon the fence.  The informatio n 
is to include a contact name, company name, and phone number for someone who can be reached 
24 hours a day, 7 days a week. 
 
7. A Floodplain Use Permit shall be obtained by the developer at the time a Building Permit is secured 
if the tower is found to be located in a Special Flood Hazard Area. 
 
8. Building Permits are required for the meteorological tower and any accessory uses (such as fences, 
etc.), and the issuance of a permit shall be acquired prior to placement of any meteorological tower. 
 
9. Prior to acquiring a Building Permit, and as development progresses, the applicant shall consult 
with and obtain written comments from the Arizona Game & Fish Department and the U.S. Fish & 
Wildlife Service (Flagstaff Ecological Services Field Office) to select a meteorological tower site 
that avoids habitat features that congregate wildlife and regarding measures to minimize the impact 
on birds and wildlife of the facilities and operations authorized by this Special Use Permit.  The 
applicant shall give due consideration to the concerns and recommendations of these agencies and 
shall give due consideration to the concerns and recommendations of  these agencies and shall use 
all reasonable efforts to satisfy their concerns and to minimize the impact on birds and wildlife, 
including the possible placement of Bird Flight Diverters on the guy wires and acoustic monitoring 
stations on or near the mete orological tower.  The location and construction of facilities shall be 
generally consistent with the Arizona Game & Fish Department’s “Guidelines for Reducing Impacts 
to Wildlife from Wind Energy Development in Arizona” and shall incorporate best availab l e 
technologies and industry practices to minimize the impact on birds and wildlife. 
 
10. Prior to acquiring a Building Permit, the applicant shall provide latitude and longitude coordinates 
(North American Datum of 1983) of the final site of the meteorological tower to the Navajo County 
Public Works Department and to the Arizona Game & Fish Department. 
 
11.  Prior to the issuance of any building permits for the meteorological tower, the developer shall

<!-- page 632 -->
submit to the Navajo County Public Works Department information regarding the manufacturer of 
the tower, structural information, information regarding who to contact in the event of an emergency 
or any problems with the tower, and information regarding how data is collected and transmitted. 
 
12. Development and use of the site shall include efforts, such Best Management Practices, to reduce 
& mitigate dust created by this project.  Efforts shall also be made to reduce the removal of 
vegetation during construction and use of the site and any roadways that provide access to the site.  
Upon the removal of the meteorological tower and associated structures and improvements, the site 
and any roadways that provide access to the site shall be restored and re -vegetated to their 
current/native condition. 
 
13. Construction of the meteoro logical tower shall commence within twelve (12) months following 
approval by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors.  Failure to do so may result in a review by 
the Board of Supervisors for possible revocation of the Special Use Permit. 
 
14. This Special Use Permit and the rights granted herein shall expire thirty-six (36) months (3 years) 
from the date of Board of Supervisors approval, unless an extension is granted by the Board of 
Supervisors upon a recommendation by the Planning & Zoning Commission.  Applica tion for an 
extension shall be made no later than 90 days before the date of expiration.  If timely application is 
made, the expiration shall be tolled until a final decision by the Board of Supervisors.  Upon the 
expiration of this Special Use Permit, the  Meteorological Tower and associated structures and 
improvements shall be removed, and the site and any roadways that provide access to the site shall 
be restored and re-vegetated to their prior condition, within 60 days.  Failure to accomplish removal 
and restoration within 60 days shall be a nuisance per se and a violation of the Zoning Ordinance, 
subject to enforcement action in accordance with Articles 24 and 27 of said ordinance. 
 
At the Planning and Zoning Commission hearing on July 16, 2020, SUP 20-003, 20-004, and 20-005 
were recommended for approval by the Commission.  This recommendation was subject to the 
conditions outlined in this report as well as the added condition that Game and Fish be contacted for 
comments to clarify if they would be i n favor or against the proposed SUPs.  Game and Fish was 
contacted and noted that they have no concerns with the proposed SUPs. 
 
PROJECT DESCRIPTION:  
 
The 198’ foot meteorological tower already exists on site.  This tower is intended to collect wind speeds, 
solar intensity, and climate related data.  This data is to be used by the owners to determine weather and 
climate patterns on the properties.  There are not currently any development plans for alternate uses of 
the property other than already existing agricultural/grazing uses.  The collection of data from the tower 
is to be used in future company analysis and land use planning. 
 
ZONING DISTRICTS: 
ARTICLE 4 – (RURAL 20) – RURAL ZONING DISTRICT TWENTY (20) ACRES PER DWELLING 
  UNIT 
 
Section 401 – Purpose. 
 
The primary purpose of this district is to conserve and protect open land uses, foster orderly growth in 
rural areas, and prevent urban agricultural land use conflicts.  The purpose of requiring large minimum 
parcels of not less than twenty (20) acres in a rea is to discourage small lot or residential subdivisions

<!-- page 633 -->
where public facilities such as water, sewage disposal, parks and playgrounds, and governmental services 
such as police and fire protection are not available or could not reasonably be made currently available.  
Uses permitted in this zoning district include both farm and non -farm residential uses, farms, and 
recreational and institutional uses. 
 
ZONING ORDINANCE: 
Article 20 – Special Uses and Planned Unit Developments 
 
Section 2001 – Special Uses  
 
14. Experimental and Proving Grounds 
 
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
 
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes 
and Article 29.  
 
This item has been noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes and Article 
29, as required. 
 
2. The proposal is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan. 
 
This proposal complies with all relevant goals and policies of Navajo County’s Comprehensive 
Plan. 
 
3. The proposed use is compatible with the permissible uses in the zoning district in which the 
property is located. 
 
The proposed use should not have incompatibility issues with other permissible uses in the zoning 
district in which the property is located based on its location. 
 
4. The proposed use is compatible with the current and likely future uses of properties in the 
vicinity of the proposed use. 
 
The properties in the vicinity of this proposal are mainly utilized for cattle grazing.  Given the size 
of these properties, as well as the subject property, and the relatively small footprint of the 
meteorological tower, th ere should be no future compatibility issues between uses on these 
properties. 
 
5. The proposed use will have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general 
welfare of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use. 
 
The proposed use should have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general 
welfare of properties in its vicinity.

Submitted via email to: 
 
Re: Aztec Meteorological Towers 1 -5, Navajo County SUP 20 -003, 20-004, 20-005, 20-006, 
and 20-007  
 
Dear Ms. Nejad, 
 
The Arizona Game and Fish Department (Department) appreciates that Navajo County reached 
out to the Department in regards to the renewal of Aztec Land and Cattle Company Ltd. special 
use permits for five Meteorological Towers (MET) in Nava jo County. The Department 
recognizes wind energy as a viable source of clean and renewable energy and with proper site 
placement and safeguards, the benefits of utilizing wind energy outweigh the potential for 
negative effect to wildlife populations. Simil arly, the Department believes that wind can be a 
viable option for energy, although specific sites may have an increased potential for negative 
impacts to certain breeding, migratory and wintering species. 
 
The Department encourages Aztec Land and Cattle C ompany Ltd, to obtain a copy of the 
Department’s wind development guidelines at: https://s3.amazonaws.com/azgfd-portal-
wordpress/PortalImages/files/wildlife/planningFor/wildlifeFriendlyGuidelines/RevisedAZWind
GuidelinesOctober2012.pdf, which provides guidance on reducing impacts to birds and bats. 
 
Department staff routinely conduct low -level aerial flight’s as part of wildlife population 
management duties, therefore the Department recommends sharing the MET tower locations 
with Department personnel when the towers are constructed and, similarly, when they are 
removed.  Having location da ta for MET tower locations will aid in allowing low -level flying 
aircraft avoid the tower. If a tower has guy wires it is recommended to have a minimum of four 
Aircraft Warning Markers (AWM’s) placed 10 meters below the apex. AWM’s should be 
recognizable f rom a distance of at least 4,000 feet (1219m) in clear air and visible from all 
directions. 
 
The Department appreciates the opportunity to provide comments on Navajo County’s Special 
Use Permitting process. The Department would like to continue to work wit h Aztec Land and 
Cattle Company Ltd and Navajo County on the scope of work moving forward. If these MET 
towers eventually indicate that a site is appropriate for wind energy development, the 
Department and the US Fish and Wildlife Service should be coordin ated  with during the early

<!-- page 636 -->
Aztec Meteorological Towers 1-5, Navajo County SUP 20-003, 20-004, 20-005, 20-006, and 20-007  
August 17, 2020 
Page 2 
planning stages to ensure that any impacts to birds, bats, wildlife migration and populations are 
avoided and/or mitigated for as much as possible. Please contact me with any questions, 
concerns, or requests for further information. Thank you. 
 
Sincerely, 
 
            Evan Lautzenheiser

Board of Supervisors Regular 5. e. 1. a.        
Meeting Date: 10/13/2020  
Title: Accept Drainage Easements within Sky Hi Retreat Lot 166 - District IV
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Sandra Phillips, Assistant County Engineer/Flood Control Manager
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Accept a perpetual Drainage Easement over and across real property in the Sky Hi Retreat
community to Navajo County for drainage, construction and maintenance purposes
Background:
Within the Sky Hi Retreat community, an existing channel conveys flows from the southeastern
corner to the northwestern portion of the subdivision and on into the forest.  A portion of this
channel is within existing drainage easements (blue hatching on overall exhibit) but there are
many gaps (purple hatching on exhibit).  The County is not able to maintain or make
improvements to this channel without these easements.  Acceptance of this easement brings us to
a total of 20 of the 24 easements needed.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year:
Budgeted Y/N: N
Amount Requested: $0
Fiscal Impact:
County staff has done field work, prepared paperwork and legals, met with and made presentations
to POA and met in the field with residents.  Staff has emphasized to the community that this is a
partnership with the County.

Board of Supervisors Regular 5. e. 2. a.        
Meeting Date: 10/13/2020  
Title: FCD Community Rating System Annual Recertification
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Public Works
Presented By: John Osgood
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and approval of Navajo County's Community Rating System Annual Recertification
Letter. 
Background:
Navajo County participates in the National Flood Insurance Program (NFIP). Compliance with
this program allows Navajo County citizens to purchase Flood Insurance underwritten by the
NFIP. Navajo County has previously elected to participate in the NFIP program known as the
Community Rating System. The Community Rating System allows communities to exceed the
minimum NFIP requirements and as a result to enjoy discounts on the Flood Insurance Premiums.
The Community Rating System requirements are focused on adopting regulatory requirements for
land use and development that exceed the NFIP minimum. Currently, Navajo County's rating has
resulted in a 10% discount in flood insurance premiums. The Flood Control team has prepared the
necessary information and is ready to submit for the annual recertification. Our submittal should
qualify Navajo County for a continued 10% reduction in Flood Insurance premiums for Navajo
County citizens.
Recommendation: 
Recommend approval.

I hereby certify that, to the best of my knowledge and belief, we are in full compliance with the minimum requirements of the NFIP and we 
understand that we must remain in full compliance with the minimum requirements of the NFIP. We understand that at any time we are not to 
be in full compliance, we will retrograde to a CRS Class 10.  
  
I hereby certify that we will continue to maintain FEMA Elevation Certificates on all new buildings and substantial improvements constructed in 
the Special Flood Hazard Area following the date at which we joined the CRS. 
  
I hereby certify that if there are one or more repetitive loss properties in our community that we must take certain actions that include 
reviewing and updating the list of repetitive loss properties, mapping repetitive loss areas, describing the cause of the losses, and sending an 
outreach project to those areas each year, and if we have fifty (50) or more unmitigated repetitive loss properties we must earn credit under 
Activity 510 (Floodplain Management Planning) for either a repetitive loss area analysis (RLAA) or a floodplain management plan (FMP). 
  
I hereby certify that, to the best of my knowledge and belief, we are maintaining in force all flood insurance policies that have been required of 
us as a condition of federal financial assistance for insurable buildings owned by us and located in the Special Flood Hazard Area (SFHA) 
shown on our Flood Insurance Rate Map. I further understand that disaster assistance for any community-owned building located in the SFHA 
is reduced by the amount of National Flood Insurance Program (NFIP) flood insurance coverage (structure and contents) that a community 
should be carrying on the building, regardless of whether the community is carrying a policy.

FEMA FORM 086-0-35A (04/17) Page 4 of 13
OMB Control Number: 1660-0022 
Expiration: 3/31/2020
Community State CID
(6-digit NFIP Community Identification Number) 
Instructions  
  
At the first verification visit after the 2013 CRS Coordinator's Manual takes effect, ONLY LINES 6 AND 13 NEED TO BE COMPLETED. These 
lines form the baseline data about the number of buildings and area of the SFHA for when the table is completed as part of the next annual 
recertification. The "period covered" entered in line 16 is the date that lines 6 and 13 are first completed.  
At all subsequent annual recertifications and cycle verification visits, the entire table is completed. The information in lines 6 and 13 from the 
last report is transferred to lines 1 and 10 in the next report.  
  
Instructions for the Columns  
  
Column A numbers are for the SFHA (the A and V Zones shown on the Flood Insurance Rate Map) (FIRM)). Use the FIRM currently in effect, 
not a draft or pending revision.  
Column B is completed only if the community receives CRS credit for regulating floodplain development outside the SFHA under Activity 410 
(Floodplain Mapping) or Activity 430 (Higher Regulatory Standards).  
Column C numbers help relate what happens in the floodplain to what is happening in the rest of the community.  
Enter "0" if there are no numbers to report for this period. Do not leave a cell blank. Do not fill in the shaded boxes.  
  
Instructions for the Lines  
  
Lines 1-7 deal with buildings.  
o   Section 301.a of the CRS Coordinator's Manual defines what constitutes a "building" and lists examples of structures that are not 
counted as "buildings" by the CRS.  
o   Section 302.a of the CRS Coordinator's Manual describes how the CRS counts buildings. For example, accessory structures are not 
counted.  
o   As noted in Section 302.a, to determine building counts, communities may use any method that yields reasonably good estimates of 
the number of buildings. Examples of acceptable methods are listed in Section 302.a. Precision is less important for large numbers. For 
example, the impact of the numbers will not change much if there are 10,000 buildings or 10,100 buildings.  
o   If a building is out of the SFHA, but in a parcel that is partly in the SFHA, it is not counted in column A --In the SFHA.  
o   In line 14, note how the building counts were obtained or estimated. Use the comments area, if needed.  
  
Line 4 refers to map revisions. These include physical map revisions, Letters of Map Revision (LOMR), and Letters of Map Amendment 
(LOMA). If a building is removed from the SFHA by FEMA through a LOMA, but the community still administers its floodplain management 
regulations on the property, the building should not be included in the line 4 count in column A --In the SFHA. However, communities that still 
regulate areas removed by LOMAs can receive credit under Activities 410 or 430. If the community is receiving such credit, the building 
should be counted under column B --In a regulated floodplain outside the SFHA.  
  
Line 7 is for the total number of buildings that were substantially improved plus the number of buildings that were substantially damaged 
during the period covered. 
  
Lines 10 -13 deal with areas.  
o   These areas are based on the areas shown on the community's FIRM including LOMRs or LOMAs. Section 403.b discusses those 
portions of the SFHA that are subtracted from the area of the SFHA to calculate the community's aSFHA used in credit calculations. 
o   Section 403.e of the CRS Coordinator's Manual discusses calculating areas for CRS purposes.  
o   Section 403.e notes that communities “should not spend an inordinate amount of time measuring areas.” As with buildings, 
communities may use any method that yields reasonably good estimates. Examples of acceptable approaches are listed in Section 403.e.  
o   Line 13 asks for the current acreage of the SFHA. The best source for this number is a GIS layer that shows the SFHA. If the 
community does not have GIS, the county, regional agency, or state NFIP mapping office may have SFHA layers and may be able to 
provide the data. If the community has a relatively recent FIRM, the study contractor or consulting engineer may have the data.  
o   In line 15, note how the area calculations were obtained or estimated. Use the comments area, if needed.  
  
Lines 17 -19 are voluntary, if the numbers are readily available.  
o   Line 17 includes replacing an existing manufactured home with a new one. The newly placed manufactured home is counted as a new, 
post-FIRM, building.  
o   The total of lines 17 -19 should equal the value entered in line 2. 
CC-213-3
